15 Gold, crude oil – at a glance

Anthony Naab, CFA 
Investment Strategist

### Page 19

Global Market Outlook 19

USD view

Further Fed tightening supports the USD near term. We expect the USD to remain supported, with our 3-month DXY forecast 
at 100.2. Resilient US payrolls for August alongside sticky energy and IT price -driven inflation reinforce the Fed's focus on 
containing inflation. Following its 25bp hike to 3.75–4.00%, we expect another hike by end-2026 and potentially another one in 
early H1 2027. Further tightening should keep US front -end yields elevated and support the USD. However, money markets 
already price a more aggressive Fed hiking path than our expectations, limiting the scope for further USD gains and leaving the 
USD vulnerable if incoming data weaken the case for additional hikes. 
Fading inflation and narrowing rate advantage point to a softer USD in the medium term. We expect the USD index (DXY) 
to decline gradually towards 98 over 12 months. Inflation pressures are expected to moderate  as the impact of oil prices and 
tariffs fade, reducing the need for continued Fed tightening. We forecast that the Fed funds rate may peak in H1 2027  in the 
current mini-rate hike cycle and then gradually decline thereafter as inflation moves closer towards target , supporting our DXY 
call. Further ECB and BoJ tightening should also progressively narrow the USD's relative rate advantage, supporting gradual 
USD moderation. Resilient US growth and elevated government bond yields should, however, limit the pace of depreciation.

Near-term EUR weakness; constructive medium term. The ECB raised its deposit 
rate 25bps to 2.50% in September. Potential ECB tightening should narrow the US-
Euro area rate differential over time, supporting EUR over 12 months.

GBP/USD 1.3219 1.33 1.35 
Rangebound with near term downside risk: UK inflation rose to 3.1% y/y, keeping 
BoE tightening risk alive, but softer labour conditions should limit the extent of 
tightening. Fed -BoE policy divergence may constrain GBP near term, while an 
expected peak in US rates by mid -2027 should allow a modes t recovery over the 
medium term. The Autum Budget in October could increase volatility.

USD/JPY 158.86 157 153 
Constructive JPY. The BoJ's September 25bp hike to 1.25% reinforces policy 
normalisation. The still wide  US-Japan yield differential may limit near-term JPY 
gains, but further BoJ tightening should gradually narrow the gap and support JPY 
over the medium term towards our target of 153.

AUD/USD 0.7012 0.73 0.74 
Near-term AUD weakness; constructive medium term: Australia’s Q2 GDP grew 
0.4% q/q, reinforcing domestic resilience. A relatively restrictive RBA stance supports 
AUD, while eventual narrowing in relative rate differentials should improve the 
medium-term outlook.

NZD/USD 0.5662 0.56 0.57 
Rangebound: The RBNZ raised rates 25bps to 2.75%, while New Zealand’s Q2 
GDP grew 0.2% q/q. Improving growth provides support, but an uneven recovery and 
near-term USD strength should limit NZD gains.

USD/CAD 1.4138 1.39 1.37 
Near-term CAD weakness; modest medium-term recovery: Canada employment 
fell 41,700 in August vs. a 15,000 expected rise, while the BoC held rates at 2.25%. 
Softer labour conditions and Fed-BoC divergence may constrain CAD near term.

USD/CHF 0.8278 0.82 0.79 
Near-term CHF weakness; medium-term CHF recovery: Swiss inflation surprised 
higher at 0.8% y/y, but the wide Fed -SNB rate differential should continue to favour 
USD near term. As Fed tightening peaks and the US rate advantage narrows, CHF 
should gradually recover over 12 months.

USD/CNH 6.7159 6.70 6.67 
Gradual CNH appreciation: China’s industrial output in August rose 5.2% y/y, 
beating estimates, but retail sales growth slowed to 0.4% on weak domestic demand. 
Production resilience and PBoC currency support favour modest CNH appreciation, 
while soft consumption and an easing policy bias should limit the pace of gains.

USD/SGD 1.2794 1.28 1.26 
Rangebound near term; constructive SGD medium term: Further Fed tightening 
and elevated US yields may limit SGD appreciation near term. Resilient 
fundamentals and MAS's exchange -rate policy should support gradual SGD 
appreciation as US rate support fades.

USD/INR 95.96 – 98 
Modestly bearish INR: India’s crude oil imports rose 26% y/y in August, and a USD 
77bn AI import deficit are both adding to structural import pressures. Higher -for-
longer US rates remain a headwind, while capital inflows and RBI intervention should 
smooth out near-term volatility and support a gradual INR depreciation towards 98.

USD/MYR 4.0865 – 4.00 Modestly bullish MYR: Resilient domestic demand, strong exports and corporate FX conversion support MYR; external uncertainty should keep appreciation gradual.

USD/KRW 1,368.9 – 1,540 
Bearish KRW: Recent KRW -supportive corporate USD selling is fading, while 
reduced National Pension Service’s FX hedging and prospect of USD 350bn US 
investment package by the South Korean national investment committee could 
increase USD demand. Higher US rates add a he adwind, supporting KRW 
depreciation over 12 months.

### Page 21

Global Market Outlook 21

Summary

Our stock-bond model (3-6 months) reduced its Overweight allocation to global equities to +1 from +3 (maximum +5) 
in September. The downgrade was driven primarily by a weaker valuation signal as valuation score fell to -2 for both Developed 
Market and Asian equities. Despite some moderation, fundamentals remain strongly supportive of equities. Positive factors 
include resilient net earnings upgrades, a positive economic surprise index, and PMI new orders remaining above 50, signalling 
continued expansion in industrial activity. Market technicals weakened but continued to support equities overall. Our market 
breadth indicator shows that 72% of equity markets remain above their 200 -day moving averages. Stock net advances are 
positive and not at levels that would typically signal an imminent market reversal. The stock-bond model continues to outperform 
strongly, generating 4.2% alpha YTD relative to the 60/40 equity-bond benchmark. 
Our 1–3 month equity market regime models continue to signal low bear market risk, with probabilities of just 0.7% for 
S&P 500 and 5.0% for MSCI AC World  indices. The low readings continued to be driven by subdued option -market implied 
volatility. At 14 – 15%, the VIX and market cap -weighted one-month implied volatility measures for Europe and US remain 
comfortably below the 20% threshold at which bear market risk tends to rise sharply. While momentum indicators have softened, 
they point to a typical market consolidation rather than signs of meaningful market stress.  
Our positioning indicators are not signalling any reversal risk. Previous long positioning in DXY has fully unwound and our 
market diversity indicator for MSCI Singapore has also normalized after the index corrected from its September peak.

15 Quant perspective: Reducing Equity 
Overweight as Valuation Weakened

Francis Lim Senior Quantitative Strategist Maggie, Au Yeung Quantitative Analyst

### Page 22

Global Market Outlook 22 
Fig. 36 Long- and short-term quantitative models remain bullish on risk assets 
Long-term models below have a typical time horizon of 3-6 months, while short-term models have a 1-3-month horizon

Long-term Stock or bond US recession model 
Current view Reduce equity Overweight 20% probability within the next 6 months 
What factors 
is this view 
based on? 
• Fundamental: +2. 3-month change in PMI new 
orders indicate a slower pace in the expansion of 
industrial activity. All other factors such as net 
earnings upgrades, economic surprise index and 
risk sentiment remain positive.  
• Valuation: -2. Both DM and Asian equities are 
expensive as their valuation scores exceeded 1 
standard deviation of historical average.  
• Technical: +1. Net-advances in global stocks are 
positive but not extreme. Market breadth declined 
as equity markets above their 200-day moving 
averages fell from 85% to 72%, but it remains 
above the model’s 60% threshold. 
Estimated probability hovers around 20 – 21% 
• Conference Board consumer confidence 
remains weak at -8.6% y/y. 
• PMI new orders-to-inventory ratio is at 1.1x, 
below its historical average of 1.2x.  
• Conference Board leading indicators 
contracted by -1.1% y/y.  
• Fed funds rate-to-natural rate ratio is still high 
at 1.6x, reflecting a restrictive monetary policy 
compared to historical average of 1.3x. 
Key model 
factors 
• Economic activity, macro risk and surprise indices, 
corporate earnings, forward P/E ratio and technical 
factors. 
• Conference Board leading indicator, Consumer 
Confidence Index, ISM New Orders and Fed 
funds rate. 
How does it 
work? 
• A monthly scorecard of -5 to 5 based on 
fundamentals, valuations and market breadth 
factors to indicate the relative preference for bonds 
and equities. A positive score favours equities and 
vice versa. 
• A probability model based on regression. Long-
term statistical tests are conducted to identify 
the four key economic factors above.

Short-term Technical analysis Investor positioning 
Current views Bullish S&P 500 & MSCI AC World indices None flagged 
What factors 
is this view 
based on? 
• Bullish S&P500 and MSCI AC World. The 
probabilities of bear market sit at just 0.7% and 
5.0%, respectively, driven by subdued options-
market implied volatility. Momentum across both 
indices have weakened but they point to a typical 
market consolidation rather than signs of meaningful  
market stress.  
• Previous long positioning in the USD index 
(DXY) has fully unwound after DXY fell 
steeply form its peak in late July. The renewed 
strength in DXY since the Fed’s 16 Sept rate 
hike has started from neutral positioning.  
• Market diversity indicator for MSCI 
Singapore has normalised after the index 
corrected from its September peak. 
Key model 
factors 
• Market factors: Momentum, volatility, interest rate 
differentials, relative returns, inflation swap rates, 
economic surprises, etc. 
• Price action: Overbought conditions occur 
when prices rise sharply; oversold conditions 
happen when prices fall rapidly in a short time.  
How does it 
work? 
• Scanning through 7,000+ factors, the framework 
uses Machine Learning to forecast market regimes 
or future trends based on identified market drivers. 
• A market indicator based on fractal analysis 
that provides a timely indication of investor 
positioning based on price actions.

Source: Standard Chartered

19 Our key forecasts and calendar events

### Page 27

Global Market Outlook 27

Time is your most precious commodity – be sure to spend it wisely 
Time is undoubtedly valuable. The days may seem long, but the years are short. So, make the choice 
to spend your time wisely. Whether you’re setting out on your investment journey, navigating the 
intricacies of mid -life wealth planning or fortifying assets  for your golden years, invest time today to 
ensure your wealth strategy is aligned to what’s right for you – Today, Tomorrow and Forever. 
Setting aside the time now to review your plan will pay dividends in the future. Markets have moved. 
Your portfolio’s current asset allocation may no longer be optimally positioned to maximise the 
opportunities ahead. Ask yourself the following: Am I holding too much cash? Am I sufficiently allocating 
to growth assets for the long term? Is my portfolio diversified? Am I capturing the best opportunities? 
And most importantly, is my wealth working hard for me so that I don’t have to? 
Use our SC Wealth Select framework and advisory specialists to help guide you through this process.

Principles 
that stand the 
test of time 
Adhering to time-tested principles, to ensure your 
investment decisions remain robust and 
consistently applied, is paramount to your 
success Today, Tomorrow and Forever. We use 
five Wealth Principles to guide and guardrail your 
wealth decisions.

Discipline – ensure consistency and 
prudence over your emotions 
• Reacting to emotions, such as optimism and 
fear, can lead to poor investment decisions at 
the worst times 
• Have a plan and stick to it – this helps you to 
stay focused on the bigger picture

Diversification – simply put, don’t put 
all your eggs in one basket 
• Reduce risk by holding a variety of financial 
assets. Multi -asset diversification in your 
Foundation portfolio is important 
• As a guide, make sure your portfolio contains 
a variety of asset classes and investments 
that have low correlation with one another

Time in the Market – a more robust 
strategy than timing the market

• Predicting market selloffs is challenging, and 
timing your exit and re-entry is difficult 
• Missing out on the best performing days of a 
market can have a significantly detrimental 
impact on your portfolio 
• ‘Time in the market’ and buying the market 
with a longer -term view provide more 
consistent returns that can ride out bumps 
along the way

Risk and Return – make sure the risk 
is worth the return 
• To achieve higher investment returns, you will 
likely have to accept a greater level of risk in 
your portfolio 
• Therefore, it’s important to understand the 
risks and manage these on an ongoing basis

Protection – don’t let the unexpected 
catch you unprepared 
• Even though you may feel healthy, or 
financially stable now, protection offers the 
ability to overcome times of financial 
uncertainty and mitigate the long-term impact 
of unforeseen events on your wealth 
• A good protection plan not only safeguards 
your wealth today, but also considers the 
value of your future earnings over your 
lifetime, in today’s terms

## Document 325: Following the oil shock, El Niño threatens to hurt macroeconomic resilience in Sub-Saharan Africa further (2026-10-21)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Following the oil shock, El Niño threatens to hurt macroeconomic resilience in Sub-Saharan Africa further (2026-10-21).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-21
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

21 October 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
FOLLOWING THE OIL SHOCK, EL NIÑO THREATENS TO HURT MACROECONOMIC RESILIENCE IN SUB-SAHARAN
AFRICA FURTHER
Lucas Plé
Seven months after the start of the war in the Middle East, which has led to a sharp rise in oil prices, sub-Saharan Africa is holding up well
overall. The deterioration in external accounts and the rise in inflation are more contained than in 2022, during the last oil shock. However,
this resilience is fragile and is eroding as the conflict drags on. Furthermore, an exceptional El Niño event could significantly worsen the

macroeconomic environment in 2027 1.
Economic growth remains robust
For countries with available data, GDP growth held up well in the first half of 2026 2. In mid-October, the IMF revised its regional forecast for
the full year upwards to 4.5% (+0.2 percentage points compared with April), in line with the performance for 2025.
1 El Niño 2026–2027: multiple pressures on prices and supplies
2 1.4% year-on-year (y/y) in South Africa, 4.2% in Nigeria, 5.1% in Kenya, 7% in Angola, 7.3% in Ghana.

Sub-Saharan Africa is highly vulnerable to climate shocks via the agricultural commodities channel

### Page 2

2
The bank for
a changing
world
Chart of the Week / 21 October 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
A mitigated shock on terms of trade
High prices for export commodities (gold, copper, platinum) have helped to cushion the impact on terms of trade and the external
accounts of hydrocarbon-importing countries. For the 10 countries in our sample (see heatmap), the depreciation of their currencies
against the US dollar since last February has been moderate overall (2.3% on average).

Inflation has started to rise again, but it is still being contained so far
In the ten countries in our sample, although inflation is rising again, it is lower year-on-year for the first eight months of 2026 than it was
for the same period in 2022. This is due to:
1. Monetary policies that were still restrictive before the conflict started;
2. Fuel subsidies;
3.   Macroeconomic reforms implemented in recent years (a return to orthodox monetary policy in Nigeria, and efforts towards fiscal

consolidation in many countries);
4. Moderate currency depreciation since February.
As a result, few central banks have tightened their monetary policy since February (South Africa, Uganda and Tanzania).
However, this resilience is still fragile…
On the one hand, governments that have chosen to cap fuel price rises are seeing their public finances deteriorate. In Senegal, energy
subsidies will go above 3% of GDP in 2026, which is unsustainable. On the other hand, the region’s external accounts are exposed to

volatile global commodity prices and tighter international financing conditions.
… and the macroeconomic environment could quickly worsen in 2027, due to an exceptional El Niño event
The region is particularly vulnerable to climate shocks via the agricultural commodities channel. Given agriculture’s high share in its GDP ,
sub-Saharan Africa is more exposed to downside risks to growth than Latin America, Asia or Central Europe (1 st column of the heatmap).

It is also more exposed than other regions of the world to the inflationary effect of rising food prices, given their higher share in the
consumer basket (2 nd column). Finally, even though its dependence on food imports is lower than in Asia and Europe (3 rd column), the
region is highly vulnerable to rising international food commodity prices due to its fragile external accounts. For several countries in our
sample, foreign exchange reserves cover less than four months’ worth of imports of goods and services (Uganda, Tanzania, Ghana and

Zambia), leaving little room for manoeuvre for absorbing a further external shock.
In addition to shocks via the agricultural commodities channel, other localised risks must be considered. In Southern Africa, El Niño is
expected to intensify droughts and threaten electricity generation as a result, particularly in Zambia (where hydropower accounts
for nearly 80% of domestic electricity generation). In East Africa, the increased flooding that El Niño is expected to cause will threaten

infrastructure – particularly logistics infrastructure – which is already less efficient than the global average 3. These two sources of
vulnerability could also increase pressure on growth, prices and the external accounts.
In conclusion, if oil prices remain high and El Niño is exceptionally severe, the region will struggle to absorb this double shock in 2027.
Despite their status as net exporters of hydrocarbons, Nigeria and Angola are also exposed to a deterioration in the macroeconomic

environment in 2027. Only South Africa appears to be better positioned to absorb the shock.
lucas.ple@bnpparibas.com
3 See the World Bank’s Logistics Performance Index.

## Document 357: Kicking and screaming_ Child care costs persist Child care remains a constraint for many families’ budgets, with the average share of monthly child care 8% of a household’s pay. 2 

Source PDF: all report pdf/Kicking and screaming_ Child care costs persist Child care remains a constraint for many families’ budgets, with the average share of monthly child care 8% of a household’s pay. 2 min read.pdf
Report source: Bank of America
Date: 2026-10-01
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

13030323     1

Economy 
Kicking and screaming: Child care costs persist 
20 October 2026

Key takeaways 
• Child care remains a growing financial burden for many households, even as growth in the median payment has moderated, 
according to Bank of America payments data. More families are paying more than $2,000 per month for care, suggesting 
affordability pressures are becoming increasingly concentrated among households facing the highest expenses. 
• Child care cost pressures vary significantly across the country. Nashville and Jacksonville, FL posted the fastest year-over-year 
growth among major metro areas, while New York and Washington DC saw the biggest decrease in median payment growth, 
according to Bank of America payments data. 
• Limited supply may continue to constrain access to child care for some households. Child care employment fell 0.7% year-over-
year in September, suggesting provider capacity remains under pressure and could continue to affect household finances and 
labor force participation.

Child care costs outpace rent and medical services 
Child care remains a significant expense for many families. In September, the growth in child care and other school-related fees 
surpassed other major household expenses such as rent and medical services, according to the Bureau of Labor Statistics (BLS) 
(Exhibit 1). 
Yet, while child care prices continue to rise in official inflation data, median household payments have largely moderated since 
February, according to Bank of America data (Exhibit 2). More importantly, the growth in the number of households paying for 
child care has fallen since the start of 2025. 
Exhibit 1: The cost of child care has surpassed other services such as 
rent in the consumer price index  
Consumer price index by category (monthly, seasonally adjusted, YoY%)

Source: Haver Analytics  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

Exhibit 2: The median child care payment per household was up 
1.0% year-over-year (YoY) in September but the number of 
households paying for child care fell 1.0% YoY 
Median child care payment per household and number of households 
(monthly, three-month moving average, YoY%)

Source: Bank of America internal data  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

Fewer lower-income households are paying for child care 
Why is Bank of America payment growth trending in the opposite direction of the Consumer Price Index? Households are likely 
changing the amount of care they use, which is reflected in slower growth in the number paying for child care. As more

households shift into lower-cost care (school-age, public pre-K, subsidized or free after-school programs), median payments can 
fall even as prices continue rising.  
The decline in the number of households paying for child care may also reflect families seeking alternatives to formal care 
arrangements. Some may be relying more heavily on relatives, shifting parents’ work schedules or dropping out of the workforce 
altogether to care for their children in response to elevated costs. This could explain why, among all income groups paying for 
care, the number of lower-income households has seen the biggest decline, falling 2.0% year-over-year (YoY) in September 
(Exhibit 3). 
Exhibit 3: The number of lower-income households paying for child care has fallen the most, down 2.0% YoY in September 
Number of households with child care payments (monthly, three-month moving average, YoY%)

Source: Bank of America internal data  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
More families face larger monthly bills 
Yet even as fewer households pay for child care, the share of those shelling out more than $2,000 per month has increased 
substantially (Exhibit 4). In fact, it has more than doubled since 2023, reaching 7.7% in 2026 year-to-date. Meanwhile, the share 
of those paying $1,000 or less has declined, suggesting higher monthly payments are becoming more common even as the 
national median grows more slowly. 
Exhibit 4: The share of households paying >$2000 a month for child care has more than doubled since 2023 
Monthly child care payments by amount (annual average, share of households, %)

Source: Bank of America internal data  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Rising costs take a bigger bite out of households’ paychecks 
What makes child care “affordable”? According to the U.S. Department of Health and Human Services, child care is considered 
affordable if it accounts for no more than 7% of family income. Yet, in Bank of America data, the average child care cost was 8% 
in September.  
While an 8% share may not appear dramatically above the federal affordability benchmark of 7%, it represents an average across 
households’ income levels. For families in high-cost metro areas or those paying more than $2,000 per month, the burden is 
likely considerably higher.

Notably, lower- and middle-income households see outsized shares of their pay dedicated to child care in Bank of America 
payments data, though this has moderated slightly from 2025 (Exhibit 5).

BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Where child care costs are climbing 
Child care pressures also differ substantially by location. In Bank of America payments data, Nashville posted the fastest YoY 
median payment growth in September, followed by Jacksonville, FL and Phoenix (Exhibit 6). This dispersion of growth across the 
country suggests child care cost pressures are no longer confined to historically expensive coastal markets.  
Interestingly, New York and Washington DC saw the sharpest decline in growth this year. Why might this be? The supply side of 
the market may help explain why – for example, New York City recently introduced universal Pre-K.1  
Exhibit 6: Nashville and Jacksonville, FL saw the greatest increase in median child care payment growth among select cities in September 
Median child care payments by core-based statistical area in September (YoY%)

Source: Bank of America internal data

Fewer providers, higher costs 
As of September 2026, employment in child care services was down 0.7% from a year earlier, suggesting the sector’s workforce 
recovery has stalled. Fewer workers can constrain the number of available places or the hours providers offer. That may leave 
families facing longer searches, fewer workable arrangements or continued price pressure. In turn, access to care can influence 
parents’ ability to enter, remain in or expand their participation in the workforce. 
Exhibit 7: Employment within child care services fell 0.7% YoY in September 
Employees by sector (monthly, seasonally adjusted, YoY%)

Source: Haver Analytics  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
What are the economic implications of rising child care costs? 
Child care costs may have important labor market implications for families with young children. Of those households paying for 
child care in 2026, more than half of them were Older Millennials (52%), followed by Younger Millennials (20%) and Gen X (16%), 
according to Bank of America payments data. This means child care costs weigh more heavily on Older Millennials. 
The share of Older Millennial households receiving more than one paycheck has continued to grow from a year earlier, but the 
pace of that improvement has moderated over the past three months (Exhibit 8). Given that this group accounts for the majority 
of households paying for child care in Bank of America payments data, they may offer early signs of how affordability affects 
their employment decisions. 
Exhibit 8: The share of Older Millennial households paying for child care that have more than one person with a paycheck has declined since 2021  
Share of households with a child care payment with more than one person with a paycheck (%, monthly, three-month moving average)

Source: Bank of America internal data  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Child care challenges can weigh on women’s employment 
According to data from the American Time Use Survey, women continue to perform significantly more child care and household 
work than men, even as their participation in paid work has increased. This suggests that persistent gaps in access and 
affordability may have disproportionate consequences for women’s employment. Indeed, the percentage of women who cited 
child care as a reason for temporary unemployment was nearly three times higher than men’s in 2026, though the average fell 
from 2025, according to data from the BLS. 
Yet labor force participation data indicates a more nuanced picture. Despite persistent child care pressures, mothers are 
participating in the workforce at rates that remain above their 2019 averages.

Mothers’ workforce participation remains elevated, but recent gains have slowed  
However, recent momentum has slowed. While mothers of young children experienced particularly large participation gains in 
2022 and 2023, those have partially reversed in 2024 and 2025 (Exhibit 10).  
In contrast, participation among women with children ages 6 to 17 has continued to edge higher. This signals that the post-
pandemic increase in working has largely been sustained in this group, but the pace of improvement has moderated, especially 
for women caring for younger children.

Methodology 
Selected Bank of America transaction data is used to inform the macroeconomic views expressed in this report and should be considered in the 
context of other economic indicators and publicly available information. In certain instances, the data may provide directional and/or predictive 
value. The data used is not comprehensive; it is based on aggregated and anonymized selections of Bank of America data and may reflect a 
degree of selection bias and limitations on the data available. 
Bank of America credit/debit card spending per household includes spending from active US households only. Only consumer card holders 
making a minimum of five transactions a month are included in the dataset. Spending from corporate cards is excluded. Data regarding 
merchants who receive payments are identified and classified by the Merchant Categorization Code (MCC) defined by financial services 
companies. The data are mapped using proprietary methods from the MCCs to the North American Industry Classification System (NAICS), 
which is also used by the Census Bureau, in order to classify spending data by subsector. Spending data may also be classified by other 
proprietary methods not using MCCs.  
If applicable, the consumer deposit data based on Bank of America internal data is derived by anonymizing and aggregating data from Bank of 
America consumer deposit accounts in the US and analyzing that data at a highly aggregated level.  
If applicable, any payments data represents aggregated spend from US Retail, Preferred, Small Business and Wealth Management clients with a 
deposit account or credit card. Any reference to aggregated spend include total credit card, debit card, ACH, wires, bill pay, business/peer-to-
peer, cash and checks.  
Unless otherwise stated, data is not adjusted for seasonality, processing days or portfolio changes, and may be subject to periodic revisions.  
Additional information about the methodology used to aggregate the data is available upon request.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
TODAY’S INSIGHTS
Financial Markets
Markets were generally buoyed overnight, with Anthropic’s
IPO filing and Meta’s US$25bn equity offering closing five
times oversubscribed adding momentum back into the
AI trade, while softer oil prices provided further support.

Attention now turns to a series of key economic developments,
with Australian CPI due today alongside policy decisions and
accompanying guidance from the RBNZ, BoC and FOMC.
• US equities moved higher, supported by AI-related
stocks. The S&P 500 gained 0.8%, while the NASDAQ
outperformed, rising 1.2%, and the Dow Jones was
unchanged. European markets were mixed, with the Euro
Stoxx 50 up 0.8%, while the FTSE 100 fell 0.8% ahead of
the UK Budget.
• Tech-heavy Asian markets were buoyed as recent headlines

helped ease concerns around AI financing. The KOSPI led
gains, rising 3.1%, while the Nikkei 225 also performed
strongly, up 1.7%. The Hang Seng diverged from regional
peers, falling 0.7%. Locally, the ASX 200 gained 0.5%.
• US Treasury yields were little changed, with the 2-year
yield rising 1bp and the 10-year yield unchanged. Futures
markets remain cautious about pricing further Fed tightening
in 2026, with the probability of a hike at today’s FOMC

meeting sitting at just 7%, taking the implied year-end fed
funds rate to 4.10%.
• Australian government bond yields were little changed
ahead of today’s CPI release, with the 3-year yield rising
1bp and the 10-year yield falling 1bp. Swap markets remain
cautious around additional RBA tightening in 2026, with the
probability of a November rate hike priced at 62%, taking
the implied year-end cash rate to 4.80%.
• Pre-FOMC positioning supported the US dollar, with

the DXY up 0.3%. The AUD outperformed other major
currencies, rising 0.2% against the greenback to 0.7028 and
also gaining across the major crosses. The euro and yen both
fell 0.3% against the USD, while the sterling declined 0.2%.
• Oil prices declined on hopes that diplomacy could avert a
renewed escalation in the US-Iran conflict. Brent fell 1.6%
to US$114/bbl, while WTI declined 1.9% to US$99.9/bbl.
Dated Brent traded at US$121/bbl. Gold rose 1.1% ahead

of today’s FOMC meeting, while positive risk sentiment
supported base metals, with copper up 1.0% and aluminium
gaining 1.5%. Iron ore fell 1.1%, remaining below US$90/t
amid persistent concerns around global demand.
International Data
US durable goods orders rose 1.8% in September after a 0.1%
gain in August. Growth for core orders (non defence, ex aircraft)
was more modest in September at 0.5% but follows a 1.6% rise
in August.
US S&P Cotality Case Shiller house prices rose a further 0.3%

in August, accelerating annual growth at the margin to 2.7%yr.
US new home sales fell 0.6% in September following a
downwardly revised 5.1% gain (prev 6.4%) in August. Sales are
currently near the average of the past 5 years.
US Conference Board consumer confidence jumped from 84.2
to 91.7 in October on an improvement in current conditions
and expectations. While still below average, this measure of
consumer confidence continues to outperform the University of

Michigan survey, held up by the robust strength of the labour
market. Responses to the Michigan measure instead look to be
focused more on cost of living.
The Richmond Fed manufacturing index was unchanged at -2
in October.
Local Data
Our New Zealand team published their 2026 General Election
preview, outlining the major parties’ key policies (see here).

## Document 378: Q3 China GDP above expectations, but downside risks remain (2026-10-30)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Q3 China GDP above expectations, but downside risks remain (2026-10-30).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-10-30
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#china #asia #aud
Q3 China GDP above expectations, but downside risks remain
By Elliot Clarke
Head of International Economics, Westpac
16:03 October 19 2026 Share
Stimulus is required if success in 2026 is to be sustained through 2027 and 2028.
China GDP surprised to the upside in Q3 2026, quarterly growth accelerating from 0.9% in Q2 to 1.2%
in Q3. Annual growth lifted from 4.3% to 4.5% as a result; though, on a year-to-date basis, growth
edged down 0.1ppts to 4.6%. The Q3 result keeps authorities on track to achieve their 4.5-5.0%ytd

ambition for 2026, and also provides a little more time to accelerate domestic demand into 2027.

### Page 2

Broad-based stimulus is required because net exports’ contribution to growth will likely abate in coming
quarters now that the trade surplus is back at record highs, and as many Chinese firms are
increasingly splitting their focus between expanding domestic capacity and offshore production
networks.

While annual retail sales growth accelerated from 0.4% to 1.1% in September, the Q3 year-to-date
growth pulse of 1.1% is just a quarter of the gain reported a year ago. Automobiles, construction
materials and jewellery have been particular heavy detractors over the past year, but most of the other
sub-categories also lack momentum. As we detailed in our September Market Outlook, this is not
because households are cash constrained, but rather as sentiment is extremely weak and the outlook
for employment and wealth highly uncertain. Key for wealth is that house prices for new and
established homes continue to decline month-on-month (respectively 0.1% and 0.3% in September)
five years into the downturn. Property investment’s ongoing decline (-20.6%ytd) suggests this
persistent weakness in sales and prices is demand driven.

While total fixed asset investment has been an outright negative for annual GDP growth since April, a
recovery is not beyond either the public or private sector’s capacity. Rather, across the economy,
entities are waiting for direction and incentive from the Central Government. This is likely to come by
early-2027 to offset the loss of support from trade, and as downside risks to global growth become
more apparent. Helpfully, industrial production continues to grow at a robust pace, 5.3%ytd, borrowing
rates for businesses and households are low, and authorities are beginning to provide banks with
additional lending capacity. For larger businesses, anecdotes suggest officials are also encouraging
major institutional investors to increase their structural allocation to domestic equities, building lasting 
appetite for new equity issuance, which in turn will fund future waves of real economic capacity and
income generation.

We remain comfortable with the view that Chinese GDP growth will hold at or slightly above 4.5%
through 2026–2028. But, the longer the Central Government waits to trigger new real investment
across both the private and public sectors, the higher the likelihood of poor sentiment becoming
entrenched and downside risks crystallising. If authorities wait until after net exports’ contribution has
faded, even holding annual aggregate growth above 4.0% could become a significant challenge. For
this reason, we expect material stimulus initiatives to be announced in the next 3 to 6 months to reset
the property sector and turn the business investment trend.

## Document 390: STABLECOINS_ What next for crypto assets_ (2026-10-20)

Source PDF: all report pdf/STABLECOINS_ What next for crypto assets_ (2026-10-20).pdf
Report source: DB Research
Date: 2026-10-20
Pages: 14
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Deutsche Bank
Research

### Page 2

Marion Laboure | Camilla Siazon |  STABLECOINS: What next for crypto assets?  |  October  20, 2026
STABLECOINS: What next for crypto assets?
Institutional adoption of stablecoins continues to incrementally progress in 2026. While
the frenzy from GENIUS has diminished, stablecoins’ links to the financial system have
continued to deepen, and more policymakers are seriously considering their use cases.
The market cap has hovered around ~$300bn over the past 12 months, a sign that the

stablecoin market is maturing. Stablecoin payments, while still marginal relative to
stablecoins' crypto-native purpose, are also growing, rising from around $103bn in
adjusted transaction volume in 2022 to $375bn in 2026 YTD.
For now, institutional adoption remains concentrated in cross-border payments, treasury
management, 24/7 liquidity provision, collateral, and real-time settlement.
The next big question is whether stablecoins will evolve to become merely a payments

technology, or a genuine disruptor to the global banking system. As we previously
established, the US promotion of stablecoins remains a geopolitical tool to ensure USD
dominance. Bloomberg also reported that the Trump administration is considering joint
ventures with private actors to promote USD stablecoins overseas, in a bid to boost the
dollar’s dominance and source demand for US Treasuries. Last week, Circle announced
plans to expand into 40 new markets in Southeast Asia, LatAm and the Middle East. 99%

of stablecoins (by market cap) remain pegged to USD and are largely backed by US
Treasuries.
Looking ahead, the key debate to watch is whether US regulations will allow for yield-
bearing stablecoins. Research shows that consumers say policymakers should prohibit
crypto companies from offering interest-like rewards for holding stablecoins, which will
threaten to siphon off the bank deposits that drive local lending. The US CLARITY Act
failed in Sep after lawmakers failed to agree on whether the broader crypto market

structure would support such yield-bearing stablecoins.
In this chartbook, we revisit the stablecoin market a year after the GENIUS Act was
signed. We look at: (i) how adoption has evolved, (ii) the emergence of stablecoins as
financial market plumbing via liquidity provisioning and collateral management, and (iii)
the key regulatory debates that will determine the industry’s next direction.
Payment volume has a +66% CAGR

### Page 3

Marion Laboure | Camilla Siazon |  STABLECOINS: What next for crypto assets?  |  October  20, 2026
REFRESHER: What are stablecoins?
– Definition: Digital tokens pegged to “stable” assets (e.g. USD or gold), issued
and transferred on a blockchain, which records ownership and transactions.
– Fiat-backed stablecoins are considered fully “collateralized”: USD
stablecoin with 1mn coins in circulation would be backed by $1mn in
cash and equivalents held in reserve by the stablecoin issuer.

– Types: Fiat-, commodity-, crypto- and algorithmic-backed.
– ~99% of stablecoins market valuation is pegged to the USD.
– Market cap: $5bn (2020) to ~$310bn (2026)
– Concentrated in USDT (~60%) and USDC (~23%).
– Benefits: 24/7 transfers & settlement, lower financial intermediaries,
programmability (e.g. escrow, conditional release, automated reconciliation),
and dollar-based store of value.
– Use cases:*
– Crypto trading (~77%) remains dominant.

– Payments (5-12%) growing, cross-border transfers and remittances.
– Store of value (17-33%) especially in emerging markets.
– Other: (~2%-8%): on/off ramps and tokenized RWA settlement.
– Alternatives: CBDCs, tokenized deposits, traditional financial architecture
*Estimates on market share taken from BCG analysis.
Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Haver Analytics, Bloomberg Finance LP. Shows stablecoin total
market cap. Updated October 192026.
Stablecoins’ reached a plateau of ~$310bn in October 2026

Marion Laboure | Camilla Siazon |  STABLECOINS: What next for crypto assets?  |  October  20, 2026
ADOPTION OVERVIEW: Most stablecoin volume is still not driven by real economic
activity
Stablecoin transaction volume:
– ~$100trn (unadjusted), ~$2.42trn (adjusted) in 2026 YTD, according to
Allium.
– Chainalysis places more bullish estimates of $28trn in 2025; projected to
reach $719trn by 2035 on organic growth alone.
Stablecoin payments:
– Stablecoin payment volume grew from $151bn in 2022 to $375bn 2026 YTD

(according to VisaOnChain Analytics).
– Businesses account for the majority (58%-64%) of payments, led by B2B
payment volumes.
– Allium Sep report showed largest business payment categories include
service fees ($56bn), payroll ($43bn) and service payments ($32bn).
– Case study: Payments volume across Visa’s >160 stablecoin-linked card
programs across consumer, business and commercial activity grew ~200%
y/y.
Strongest adoption in remittances, B2B payments and dollar access in countries

with unstable currencies or weak banking systems.
– Benefits for cross-border use cases: Instant settlement, <$0.10 per
transaction, borderless, transparent, 24/7, no intermediaries. Remittance fees
are lower (0.5-3.0%) than the global fee average (~6.6%).
– Stablecoins represented 0.31% of fiat cross-border payment value in 2025.
Marginal portion but registered +64% growth increase vs fiat (+9%) according
to Allium. B2B payments had the highest cross-border share (43%).

– A 2025 IMF study also shows transaction flows reached  ~7.7% of GDP in
Latin America and ~6.7% in Africa and the Middle East.
Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Allium. Shows top 8 countries by received payments from Jan to August 2026.
Only the receiving end is attributed, so a $100 payment counts once, not twice. That attribution covers 13.5% of
stablecoin payments.

CONSUMER/RETAIL PAYMENT: Still on the periphery
Consumer payments: Not yet there
– Retail adoption is still the most distant use case, especially amongst developed-market consumer credit card spend. A recent ABA survey found that only
24% of consumers think that stablecoins could provide meaningful benefits.
– Stablecoins still look more like a debit instrument than a credit product. They do not naturally replicate the consumer valueproposition of credit cards (i.e.

rewards, revolving credit, dispute rights, chargebacks, fraud protection and convenience), so there is limited incentive for developed-market consumers to
use them.
– Limited scope for payments also evident in wallets (see chart below). Very little of the stablecoin supply is being held as balances in consumer wallets.
Still, the number of retail payments is incrementally growing, with cross-border, emerging market use cases strongest
– Retail transaction volume: $2.6bn (2020) to $71bn (2024); $66bn YTD in 2026.*

– Strongest use in: (i) economies with hyperinflation; (ii) poor banking access; or (iii) high remittance fees.
Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Allium data.  Shows circulating supply by holder type (Sep 2026). Categories calculated as a percentage of total circulating supply. Note that wallets account for money being held vs
payments (money moving). Business wallets in this case refer to those holding smaller balances with quick turnover (i.e. payment services, merchants, gaming), while institutional wallets hold larger balances and turn them

over quickly, i.e. financial-services firms, treasuries, and wallets. Note: *Retail sized transactions are defined as transactions of $250 or less, according to Visa OnChain Analytics.
The amount of stablecoin supply in consumer wallets stands at ~14%

### Page 6

Marion Laboure | Camilla Siazon |  STABLECOINS: What next for crypto assets?  |  October  20, 2026
CASH/LIQUDITY MANAGEMENT: Stablecoins are evolving as a major tokenized
contender
Sources: Deutsche Bank Research, taken from DB’s Tokenised money and the future of FXand Stablecoins and
agentic commerce: new rails, old frictions.
Stablecoins can help treasury management and settlement
– Stablecoins have a strong use case for settling transactions for FX,

treasury operations and collateral management i.e. intra-day repos.
– This is because stablecoins’ 24/7 settlement can address
operational frictions in cross-border transactions.
Poses significant risk to cross-border B2B transactions for banks
– Compared to traditional banks, stablecoins offer substantial relief
from transaction costs. Our equity analysts estimate total customer
processing fee savings to increase from 0 to ~$70bn in 2035, if
consumers switch from traditional banks to stablecoins.

Stablecoins is not a replacement solution to FX settlement risk
– FX settlement requires two currencies to be exchanged
simultaneously. Currently, stablecoins only serve as an intermediary
between the two currency legs, resulting in a “stablecoin sandwich”.
– Overwhelming concentration of USD-stablecoins also limits
stablecoin ability to support widespread payment-versus payment
settlement across currency pairs.
– Stablecoins also face fragmented interoperability/liquidity issues

and regulatory uncertainty across issuers/networks/jurisdictions.
– For more, read our FX report here.

### Page 7

Marion Laboure | Camilla Siazon |  STABLECOINS: What next for crypto assets?  |  October  20, 2026
INSTITUTIONAL ADOPTION: Corporates, card networks, and banks are integrating
stablecoins
Institutions are racing to capture stablecoin market share
– Card networks: Mastercard expanded its settlement capabilities to include
stablecoins, using USDC and Paxos-issued stablecoins.
– Institutions: Exploring stablecoins via developing in-house permissioned

blockchain experiments and/or exploring issuance of proprietary stablecoins.
– >140 companies and financial institutions helped launch Open USD
(OUSD) in Sep 2026. OUSD reached $799mn of supply in 2 weeks.
Signals shift toward consortium-backed/network-led digital dollar
models.
Are stablecoins posing a threat to traditional payment systems?
– Bullish: Stablecoins to surpass traditional rails by the 2030s (Chainalysis).
– ~17% of stablecoin-linked card volume is occurring across Visa’s

business and commercial card programs. Shows growing adoption of
stablecoins for settlement, treasury management and cross-border
commerce.
– Key concerns for banks: Fee pressure from stablecoin adoption →
bank outflows → higher reliance on wholesale funding → pressure on
net interest margins.
– Base case: However, Deutsche Bank equity analysts see more limited near-
term risk. Visa and Mastercard to remain dominant especially in advanced
markets.

– KYC, AML, sanctions screening, wallet monitoring, fraud controls and
jurisdiction-specific limits remain essential for banks.
Stablecoin transactions could match Visa and Mastercard transactions
sometime between 2031 and 2039 (Chainalysis).
Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Chainalysis.
Shows projected years until crypto merchant transactions reach Visa scale.

### Page 9

Marion Laboure | Camilla Siazon |  STABLECOINS: What next for crypto assets?  |  October  20, 2026
GEOPOLITICS: Why stablecoins are a "Genius" idea for the US
Source: Deutsche Bank Research, US Treasury, Tether July 2026 report. Shows Major foreign holders of Treasury Securities.
Treasury data from June 2026.
Can sustain USD dominance
– 99% of stablecoins (by market cap) are USD-pegged.
– Stablecoins function primarily as a digital version of dollars

moving over blockchain rails, extending the dollar’s reach into
new digital environments.
– Reinforces dollar savings: The more firms invoice and transact in USD,
the more they save in USD assets. Stablecoins help preserve this
feedback loop by keeping businesses and individuals in dollar
payment rails.
Can support US debt, given share held by foreigners has fallen from
>50% in early 2000s to ~30% in early 2026.
– Stablecoin issuers total holdings: ~$170bn according to Allium x

Citizen.
– Tether: ~$141bn in direct and indirect exposure to US T-bills.
– Impact: BIS shows how $3.5bn inflow into dollar-backed stablecoins
lowers 3m T-bill yields by 2.5-3.5bp under normal conditions. Effect
more than doubles in periods of T-bill shortages.
– Forecasts: T-bills holding by stablecoin issuers could reach ~$1trn by
2028 (Treasury forecasts).
– Recent Fed SF research places it closer to $400bn by the end
of 2030, lower than the US government’s financing needs.

Stablecoin issuers’  demand for US debt could counterweight declining demand from China
Tether competes with Saudi Arabia and South Korea as major holders of US Treasuries
Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Federal Reserve Bank of San Francisco, US Treasury Data. Shows Treasury
holdings, stablecoin issuers vs China.

### Page 10

Marion Laboure | Camilla Siazon |  STABLECOINS: What next for crypto assets?  |  October  20, 2026
US GENIUS ACT: Debate on rules around payment stablecoins one year on
GENIUS provisions
– Strict issuer requirements: Issuers can be private, non-bank entity, but must
meet strict federal or state requirements.
– Reserves: Requires 1:1 backing with US cash, 93 days T-bills, repos/reverse
repos, or money market funds holding those assets.
– AML/KYC/BSA compliance obligations to stablecoin issuers, aligning them

exams, and strong internal controls.
Next steps
– GENIUS takes effect no later than 18 Jan 2027, or 120 days after final
implementing regulations are issued.
– Ongoing pushback from US banks as to whether GENIUS ban on yield-
bearing stablecoins should extend to affiliate or third-party arrangements,
given many users do not hold stablecoins directly with the issuer.
– CLARITY ACT failed in Sep because of disagreements on how
exchanges/broader crypto market structure should handle stablecoins.

– The US Treasury has proposed implementing rules for payment stablecoins.
There are two ways stablecoin yield can be paid; ongoing debate over the three-
party model
Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Congressional Research Service.

RISKS: Still hotly discussed amongst policymakers and financial institutions
Bank liquidity risk
Loss of stable funding source for banks from deposit outflow (not offset by
partial flowback from stablecoin issuers’ reserve deposit)
System stability risk
Stablecoins can fail to maintain their $1 value during times of stress. Evidence shows
that USDC and Tether actually trade within 1% of their dollar value but prices can

diverge sharply during stress episodes.
Run on stablecoin issuer(s) in market stress / trust scenario triggering fire sales of
reserve assets and/or deposit withdrawals.

stablecoins, e.g. gold with only

REGULATORY COMPARISON
Overview of stablecoin regulation in the US, UK and EU UKEU US
Regime Maturity
Key Timelines
Regulatory
Framework
US
The U.S. Genius Act
EU
Markets in Crypto-Asset Regulation
(MiCAR)
Backing Assets*
• 100% backing assets in USD cash or short-term
Treasuries (≤93-day T-bills).
• Non-significant EMTs –At least 30% reserves in
same-currency bank deposits, remainder in HQLA

• Significant EMTs - At least 60% of the reserves in
bank deposits; remainder in HQLA
• Systemic stablecoins – 30% unremunerated BoE
deposits vs 70% in UK sovereign debt (HQLA)
• Non-systemic – Full 1:1 backing in HQLR assets,
minimum 5% in on demand deposits.
UK
Multi-issuance
Stance
• Rules are still being fine-tuned; no specific focus
yet on multi-issuance.
• Multi-issuance under active review; EC considers multi-
issuance permissible, an equivalence-based framework for

EU/third-country arrangements is under legislative
consideration.
• BoE considers multi-issuance unsuitable for systemic
UK use. Preference for single-issuance models backed
by single reserve (incl. Non-GPB denom. stablecoins) .
UK Cryptoasset Regime (FCA)
UK Systemic Stablecoins Regime (BoE)
Eligible Issuers
• Bank subsidiaries, federal nonbank licensees, or
state issuers with federal registration, with
enhanced oversight for >$10bn circulation.

• EMTs: EU-authorised credit institutions or authorised
electronic money institutions. Restrictions apply to
non-EU currency EMTs (exceeding MiCAR
thresholds) as a means of exchange
• FCA-authorised firms for non-systemic stablecoins,
• Systemic issuers to be designated by HM Treasury
and jointly regulated under BoE/FCA oversight
Balance Sheet
Treatment
• Stablecoins treated as reserve-backed digital
payment assets, segregated in bankruptcy-remote

accounts and treated as cash equivalents
• EMTs are fiat-referenced cryptoassets subject to
MiCAR and e-money requirements
• (OCC/FDIC) Mandates prudential capital buffers
tailored to issuer risk profile.
• (Fed) Framework consistent with bank requirements
• EMI issuers: minimum own funds of 2% of EMT
supply; 3% for significant EMTs. Significant issuers
subject to enhanced risk-management requirements
• Systemic: 6 months operating expenses/largest

plausible loss event
• Non-systemic: 1% av. stablecoins in circulation
Capital Reserve
Requirements**

《國防需求已由短期補庫存轉為多年期擴產》
美國主要航空與國防類股公布3Q26財報，中東衝突
再升溫導致國防需求高於商業航空 。以下是主要廠
商的分析 :
雷神∶受益於飛彈回補庫存 ，3Q26取得超過810億
美元商用與國防新訂單 ，其中國防訂單 590億美元 ，
包括約 230億美元 「戰斧 」飛彈訂單與 140億美元
「先進中程空對空飛彈 」（AMRAAM）訂單 ，帶
動總積壓訂單較上季增加 520億美元 ，累計訂單
3,400億美元 ，年增36%。在飛彈防空系統與商業
航空維修服務市場強勁帶動下 ，公司上調2026年營
收到973億美元、EPS 7.70美元(年增22%)。

洛克希德馬丁 ∶受益於飛彈與火控系統產量提升 ，
帶動3Q26營收與獲利優於預期。在PAC-3多年期合
約則推升下，積壓訂單增加616億美元達2,920億美
元。由於美國 、中東、北約國家飛彈補庫需求將持
續數年，所以公司上調2026年營收到810億美元(年
增8%)、EPS 30.55美元(年增42%)。
奇異航空 ∶受益於 LEAP交付 、備件 、維修及 shop
visit增加帶動 ，商用發動機及服務營收年增 21%，
3Q26營收優於預期。在商用航空備件、維修服務及
引擎交付需求優於預期 ，加上主要供應商材料投入
改善 ， 公司上調 2026 年營收到 508億美元 、EPS
7.75美元(年增26%)。
總結，雖然國防類股近期財報受益於產能提升維幅
上調全年財測 ，反觀油價高漲對於美國航空運輸業
造成全面性的衝擊，所以工業維持中立評等。

重點摘要 左右市場表現 ，這將體現在高低評等發行人不僅基
本面的分化在高利環境下將更明顯 ，價格表現也將

《非投資級債信用基本面大致穩健，但分歧加大》
第二季美非投資級債發行人獲利狀況強勁 ，除交通
運輸、住房、電信和有線電視產業外 ，所有產業的
EBITDA(息稅折舊攤銷前淨利 )皆有成長，債務槓桿
出現六季以來首次下降 ，利息覆蓋率也提升至兩年
來的最高水準 ，但槓桿率和利息覆蓋率仍分別比指
數長期平均高出 0.17倍以及低 0.26倍，主要原因為
總債務成長 ，以及高利率環境下再融資成本更高 ，
令利息成本上升 。此外美非投資級債發行人過去 12
個月的資本支出年增幅創2023年第三季以來的最高
水準，主要由科技業帶動；不過在整體穩健的資產

負債表狀況中仍有 K型分化的情形 ，如43%的發行
人利息覆蓋率超過五倍 ，此比率為兩年新高 ，但同
時有21%的發行人利息覆蓋率低於 2倍，這也創了
近五年新高 ，反映在高利環境下債信基本面分化加
大，雖然目前違約狀況持穩 ，整體美非投資級債發
行人債信基本面在獲利支持下仍穩健 ，不過在利率
將在高檔區間維持一段時間的預期下 ，基本面的分
化有機會進一步加大 ，此外觀察過去歷史數據顯示
利息保障倍數與利率環境密切相關 ，如美歐信用債
利息保障倍數雖在2022年疫後復甦時期創近年高點 ，
但隨後又隨著利率走高而回落 ，因此儘管近期如上
述利息保障倍數出現小幅改善跡象 ，但基於美歐公
債利率的上升將增加債券再融資帶來的償債成本壓
力，預期債信指標未來也將承壓。

熱門議題 / 投資關鍵
《地緣風險衝擊中東信用基本面 ，但緩衝能力仍佳 》
中東戰事持續外擴 ，除了美伊相互襲擊使得荷姆茲
海峽情勢依然緊張外 ，葉門胡塞武裝也對沙烏地阿
拉伯密集發動攻勢 ，柴油出口因此受阻 ，能源價格
處高位不墜 ，布蘭特原油 10月以來便維持在 100美
元/桶之上。過往中東國家往往是高油價的受惠者，
但受到能源生產和運輸中斷衝擊 ，國際貨幣基金10
月下調2026年中東及中亞地區經濟成長率預估值 1
個百分點至衰退，沙烏地阿拉伯更預估將衰退 3.3%。

人民幣債指數 2.20% 景氣不均衡復甦，寬鬆貨幣政策為主要支撐力

重點摘要 沙烏地阿拉伯 (以下簡稱沙國 )雖極力避免直接捲入
戰爭，但仍遭受衝突外溢效應波及 。戰爭不僅導致
沙國大幅增加補貼 ，軍事和安全支出以及融資費用
上升均加大財政支出 ，因此，即便油價上漲抵消產
量下降的影響而使石油收入高於預期，沙國2Q26預
算赤字佔 GDP比重仍達 7.1%，遠高於年度目標的
3.3%。儘管近期公布的 2027年預算計畫 (PBS)目標
是將預算赤字佔 GDP比率從2026年的4.9%(沙國官
方最新預估 )縮小到2027年的3.6%，但若戰爭長時
間 延 續， 此 目 標 或 難 以 達 成， 目 前 估 計2027 年
17420億里亞爾的債務目標亦將輕易被超越 。不過
即使如此 ，沙國目前負債占 GDP比重僅約 35%，

2027年市場最悲觀預期也大約40%左右，仍是低於
全球新興國家均值約80%的水準。
中東另一主要發債國卡達對天然氣出口依賴度高 ，
天然氣出口受阻也使得國際機構普遍預估卡達 2026
年經濟衰退10%以上、幾乎是2010年以來最重，所
幸卡達2026年以前累積巨額經常帳盈餘及外匯儲備
作為緩衝，即使在今年3月外部環境面臨極大壓力下，
卡達仍有 199億里亞爾的經常帳盈餘 ，累積過去四
季經常帳占 GDP比重仍達 13.5%，現今外匯存底足
以彌補3月的貿易逆差，並可維持４年以上。這些數
據都顯示戰爭對中東國家的經濟及對外償債能力確
實已帶來短線壓力 ，但過去累積的龐大緩衝使其具
備良好因應能力 ，信用違約交換 (CDS)目前並未因
定價油氣供應長期中斷而大幅攀升 ，今年雖持續發
債但債務壓力並不高 ，IMF更預期2027年中東國家
經濟將成長6.3%。然中東國家對能源出口依賴度仍
高，能源出口是否長期受阻則是後續觀察重點。

資結果自行負責。未經本公司許可，本資料及訊息不得逕行抄錄、翻印或另作派發。本行以上市場資訊與分析，不涉及特定投資標的之建議。
重點摘要 《經濟強勁帶來的高利率無損新興債利差穩步收斂 》
財政收支並非驅動新興主權信用利差的唯一因素 ，
總體經濟環境 、市場流動性均牽動利差走勢 ，因此
雖然自7月以來沙烏地阿拉伯等中東國家信用利差又
再擴大，但也大概在3年均值附近並不算高。以歷史
經驗來看，BB級主權國家利差通常對財政惡化程度
反應較大 ，可能是因為該評等僅低於投資等級一級 ，
財政變化容易牽動投資人對升等至投資級的預期與
投資部位的調整 ，而在投資等級的主權國家中 ，則
以匈牙利和波蘭這些債務負擔較重的主權債利差較
易受財政問題影響。
油價和利率是近期2項重要觀察變數，也牽動利差走
勢，不過由於新興主權債指數中約5成的組成為大宗
商品出口國、3成為石油出口國，反而能受惠油價上

漲，油價上漲時與利差走勢敏感度也較油價下跌時
低；然而 ，更重要的是高油價對通膨和政策應對的
二階影響 ，將導致高利率長時間延續的可能性並最
終衝擊實體經濟 ，進一步限制石油出口國利差收窄 。
以長期趨勢來看 ，利率水準的絕對值高低不必然影
響新興債利差 ，利率走勢反而和信用利差呈現小幅
負相關，利率走升反映的若是強勁經濟成長而非能
源供給衝擊 ，此時往往能伴隨信用利差收窄 ，反而
需留意的是利率波動放大及利率的大幅重新定價 ，
這才是牽動利差波動加大的主因 。目前總體經濟環
境部分或可與2004~2007年相比擬，當時在強勁成
長的背景下利率長時間在4%~5.3%高位震盪，但新
興主要貨幣主權債利差仍可收斂至 134點(此波低點
189點)，若經濟成長能在現今高油價和高利率環境
下持續強勁，利差則可望保持穩定。
重點摘要
• BB級主權債及債務負擔沈重
的投資級主權債利差較容易
受財政問題干擾
• 新興主權債指數中約 5成為石
油出口國 ，降低油價上漲對
利差走勢的敏感性
3
即使油價上升，新興債利差與油價也少見正相關

重點摘要 《景氣及企業獲利續成長 ，企業債信改善趨勢延續 》
三大信評機構持續認可新興市場四大區域企業信用
評等的改善，3Q26新興市場企業債淨升等規模大幅
提高至760億美元，其中獲上調規模達到910億美元、
創下2025年第四季以來的最高水平 ，也已是連續第
5個季度改善。
3Q26新興市場4大區域評等調升數量均高於下調 ，
其中以亞洲改善動能最為顯著 ，主要貢獻來自於亞
洲金融業 ，全年升評 /降評比率已來到 1.8倍，逆轉
2019年降評多於升評的劣勢；拉美企業仍是新興市
場中違約率最高的區域 ，降評債券數量與其他區域
比也確實較高 ，但降評企業家數已是過去 10年來最
低，加上阿根廷主權債信獲上調帶動企業債 (主要是
國有企業)信評調升，拉美航空業者如南美航空集團

(LATAM Airlines)在油價維持高位環境下仍於3Q26
獲信評機構調升至 BB+，在企業債信分化環境下仍
有亮點 ，因此第三季拉美企業淨評級上調規模達
370億美元，反而創下2025年第三季以來的最佳季
度表現 ，全年共有 234家拉美企業信評得調升 。新
歐中東地區債信今年算是少數債信惡化的區域 ，很
大部分是受到戰爭及高油價影響所致 ，不過奈及利
亞和南非等主權評級上調仍帶來正向效果 ，梯瓦製
藥(Teva)評等更是上調至投資等級，帶來了120億美
元的貢獻，部分抵消地緣政治風險帶來的影響。
強勁的獲利成長持續支持新興企業穩健基本面 ，有
限的違約率輔以韌性十足的總經環境 、以及活躍但
不過度集中的初級市場 ，均是支持債信改善的原因 ，
預期在企業獲利仍看好的情境下 ，第四季企業債信
仍將朝正向發展。

重點摘要 《聯準會升息年 ，新興債雖有波動但正報酬機率高 》
觀察過往聯準會升息年度 ，利率溫和走升機率相對
較高，新興債市雖然多會受到升息壓力而出現一波
跌勢，但全年度仍有較大機率收紅 ，1996年以來僅
有2018、2022這兩個升息年度新興債市全年收黑 。
不過2022年新興債市重挫很大一部分原因來自於聯
準會在短短一年時間內將基準利率自0％升至4.25%，
這種猛爆性升息與目前所預設的風險管理式的溫和
升息環境有所不同；另外 ，2018年聯準會雖只升息
四碼，但土耳其及阿根廷因美元稀缺引發流動性風
險，這2國經常帳赤字占 GDP比重當時均高於 5%，
對外融資壓力上升的同時進而爆發金融危機 。然而
現階段新興國家經常帳已普遍改善 ，土耳其經常帳

赤字已降至 2%~3%，阿根廷更轉為順差 ，基本面
與2018年有很大的差異。若扣除這兩段期間，新興
債市在聯準會升息期間波動雖可能放大 ，但在貨幣
政策方向更趨明朗後 ，走勢便會止穩反彈 ，全年平
均報酬率仍有10%以上。
至於新興市場企業債部分 ，過去因升息所造成的跌
勢約是主權債的一半 ，主要是因為企業債發行天期
多較主權要來得短 ，加上該期間獲利動能強勁 ，因
此在利率上升時期表現往往相對主權債更具韌性 。
儘管利率走勢仍存不確定性，但最終若能穩定在5%
左右，預估新興企業債全年總報酬仍可維持在3%，
除非未來出現更大的外部衝擊 ，如利率持續大幅上
升引發對經濟成長受損的擔憂 ，或AI驅動的成長模
式被重新評價 ，否則對新興債市的看法仍將維持正
向，新興主要貨幣主權債在這樣的大環境下 ，短線
雖相對承壓，但後續反彈力道也可望大於企業債。
• 新興主要貨幣主權債在聯準
會升息年度正報酬機率近8成
• 新興企業債相對抗跌 ，公債
利率若穩定在5%左右，全年
總報酬仍可維持在3%

（注）  作成時点で、 第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所経済調査部が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、 その正確性、 完全性に対す
る責任は負いません。
米国中間選挙は、 大統領任期4年の中間である2年目に行われる連邦議会選挙です。 2026年11月3日に実施され、 下

院の全435議席と上院の約3分の1が改選対象となります。 政権に対する 「中間評価」 と しての意味合いを持ち、 今後の
政策運営に大きな影響を与えます。
本選に先立って行われた各党の候補者選び （予備選挙） では、 トランプ大統領の後押しを受けた共和党候補が相次い
で勝ち上がった一方、 民主党でも現職政権への対決姿勢を鮮明にする候補が選ばれるなど、 両党ともに熱心な支持層
の意向が強く出た結果となりました。 しかし本選に向けては、 高止まりする物価や生活費負担への不満を背景に トランプ
大統領の支持率が低下傾向にあると報じ られるなど、 政権側には逆風も吹いています。 有権者の間では、 長引く中東情
勢の悪化を受けたインフレや雇用環境といった 「生活防衛」 が最大の投票判断基準となっているほか、 国境管理 ・ 不法移
民問題や人工妊娠中絶の権利といった社会的テーマも、 勝敗の鍵を握る激戦区 （スイング ・ ステー ト） での浮動票の行方
を左右する重要な争点です。
執筆時点の情勢と しては、 上院は共和党優位、 下院は接戦ながら民主党がやや有利とみられています。 先行きへの影

響と しては、 共和党が上下両院の過半数を維持する 「 ト リプルレ ッ ド」 が続けば、 トランプ政権の政策実行力が高まり規制
緩和などが進みやすくなる一方、 関税引き上げや財政悪化への懸念が残ります。 反対に、 民主党が下院を制して 「ねじれ
議会」 となった場合は、 法案や予算が通りにく くなり政権運営が停滞する反面、 過度な強硬策にブレーキがかかる点がか
えって安心材料と受け止められる側面もあります。 中間選挙の結果は、 米国の国内政治にとどまらず、 今後の通商政策
や金利の先行きを見極める分岐点となります。 （主席エコノミス ト ：  阿原健一郎）

## Document 498: インドの現金社会に決済革命～UPI急成長の背景と新たな課題～

Source PDF: all report pdf/インドの現金社会に決済革命～UPI急成長の背景と新たな課題～.pdf
Report source: NLI
Date: 2026-10-28
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

1――現金社会を変えたUPI革命 
インドの街角では、 露店や小さな商店にもQR
コード1が掲げられ、スマートフォンで代金を支
払う光景が広がっている。2017年には取引件数
の約９割を現金が占めていたという2。わずか10
年ほどで、支払いの常識は大きく変わった。そ
の中心にあるのが、2026年８月に開始10周年
を迎えた統一決済インターフェース（UPI）で
ある。インド決済公社（NPCI）が運営するUPI
は、銀行口座間で24時間 365日、即時に送金・
決済できる仕組みだ。利用者は店頭のQRコー
ドを読み取り、支払いを承認すれば、相手と銀行や決済アプリが異なっても支払える（図表２）。

1 「QRコード」は株式会社デンソーウェーブの登録商標である。 
2  インド政府広報局（2023年 10月 30日） 「India’s UPI: A global front-runner in digital payment systems」。

### Page 2

2――UPI普及の経済的意義 
UPIの普及は、日々の経済活動の効率化にもつながる。現金の管理や釣り銭の準備にかかる手間が
減るほか、代金を即時に受け取れるため、カード決済のような入金待ちがなく、仕入れなどの運転資
金に充てやすい。こうした取引の手間や入金までの時間の削減が、小規模商店の業務や資金繰りを支
える。4 
また、決済履歴の蓄積は金融アクセスの改善にも寄与し得る。日々の取引履歴が信用審査に活用さ
れれば、 十分な借入実績や担保を持たない個人や小規模事業者も融資を受けやすくなる可能性がある。
決済の効率化に加え、金融サービスの利用機会を広げることもUPIの重要な経済的意義である。

3 DBTは Direct Benefit Transfer、PMJDYは Pradhan Mantri Jan Dhan Yojanaの略。従来も有権者IDなどを使って銀
行口座を開設できたが、銀行支店へのアクセスや本人確認手続きの負担があった。ジャン・ダン計画では、最低残高を必要
としない基本預金口座を提供し、銀行代理人を通じた口座開設やアドハーによる電子的な本人確認（e-KYC）を推進した。 
4 RBI（2022）Discussion Paper on Charges in Payment Systems.

### Page 3

3――各国との比較でみるUPIの特徴 
UPIは、Google Payや PhonePeなど異なる
アプリを通じ、利用者と加盟店が別の銀行口座
を使っていても支払える。こうした相互運用性
が UPIの普及を支えてきた。6日本の「ことら
送金」も銀行やアプリを超えた共通基盤だが、
10万円以下の個人間送金が対象で、UPIのよう
に一般の店舗決済まで広く扱うものではない。 
共通の即時決済基盤を店舗決済にも活用する
のが、ブラジルの「Pix」とタイの「PromptPay」
である。Pixはブラジル中央銀行が2020年に導
入し、PromptPayはタイの銀行業界が共同開発して2017年に始まった。 
2025年の人口１人当たり年間取引件数は、インドの156件に対し、ブラジルは374件、タイは383
件となる（図表５） 。ただし、所得水準や銀行口座の普及率、取引内容などが異なるため、件数の差だ
けで普及余地は判断できない。インドでは今後、利用者層の拡大に加え、１人当たりの利用頻度を高
めることが普及の鍵となる。 
費用負担の仕組みにも違いがある。インドでは銀行口座を通じたUPIの個人間送金（P2P）は無料
であり、2020年１月からは店舗決済（P2M）の加盟店手数料（MDR）もゼロとしてきた。政府は銀
行や決済事業者の負担を軽減するため、2021年度から少額の店舗決済などを対象とする奨励金制度
を実施している。

5 Monetary Authority of Singapore（2023年 2月 21日）“Launch of Real-time Payments between Singapore and 
India”. 
6 Copestake, Kirti and Martinez Peria（2025）Growing Retail Digital Payments: The Value of Interoperability, IMF 
Fintech Note

### Page 4

4――無料モデルの見直しと今後の課題 
利用者が無料で使えても、決済の処理や不正対策、システムの維持・拡充に必要な費用がなくなる
わけではない。政府の奨励金による支援には限界があり、安定的な財源の確保が難しく、長期的な技
術投資を制約するおそれがある。無料化によって参加者が増えれば決済基盤の価値は高まる一方、そ
の便益を維持するには継続的な投資の財源が必要となる。 
こうしたなか、政府は2026年９月、2,000ルピーを超える一部の店舗決済に原則0.4％の加盟店手
数料（MDR）を導入する方針を発表した。7個人間送金や少額の店舗決済、所定の小規模商店が受け
取る決済は無料を維持し、店舗決済の約96％（件数ベース）は引き続き対象外となる。当初10月 15
日に予定された導入は、祭礼期に配慮してディワリ後に延期され、実施日は別途通知される。8 
また、MDR総収入の５％相当を、小規模加盟店の利用を支援する基金に充てる方針も示された。
一部の加盟店から費用を回収する一方、その収入を小規模商店などへの普及支援に活用することで、
決済基盤の維持と利用拡大の両立を図る狙いがある。 
UPIの普及は、現金に依存してきた日々の取引を効率化し、金融サービスへのアクセスを広げる可
能性も持つ。今後は、加盟店の負担増による利用の停滞を避けながら、手数料収入を決済基盤の安定
的な運営につなげられるかが焦点となる。無料モデルの見直しは、UPIがもたらしてきた経済的なメ
リットを持続させるための転換点といえる。

7 インド財務省（2026年 9月 15日）“UPI Continues to Remain Free for Peer to Peer Transactions and 96% of 
Merchant Transactions”, Press Information Bureau. 
8 The Tribune（2026年 10月 13日）“UPI fee row: Govt defers 0.4% MDR on select merchant transactions till after 
Diwali”. 
本資料記載のデータは各種の情報源から入手・加工したものであり、その正確性と完全性を保証するものではありません。 
また、本資料は情報提供が目的であり、記載の意見や予測は、いかなる契約の締結や解約を勧誘するものではありません。

## Document 557: 債券市場總評：投等債吸引力增

Source PDF: all report pdf/債券市場總評：投等債吸引力增.pdf
Report source: Fubon
Date: 2026-10-20
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

1

台北富邦銀行 
Oct 20，2026 | 2:00 PM (GMT+8) 
債券市場總評： 投等債吸引力增
投資研究部 
 Research 
Financial Market 
▎通膨壓力續高，年底升息預期仍存 
10 月上旬以來，美債殖利率自高檔回落，高殖利率逐
步吸引買盤，股市震盪亦帶動避險資金回流債市。不
過，受燃料與能源價格上揚帶動，9 月 CPI 年增率由
3.4%升至 3.7%，PPI 年增率亦由 5.6%升至 6.3%，核
心 CPI 與核心 PPI 年增幅也高於預期，顯示物價壓力
仍高。加上零售銷售月增 0.8%優於預期、初領失業金
人數維持低位，反映消費與勞動市場仍具韌性，市場
因此雖傾向 Fed 本月先按兵不動，但 12 月再升息 1
碼的預期仍存。 
圖 1  9 月 CPI、PPI 年增率同步走高

資料來源：Bloomberg，2026/10/19 
▎避險買盤增溫 ， 殖利率回落、債市修復 
近期美債買盤回升也反映在公債標售結果，3 年、10
年及 30 年期標售需求皆表現穩健或強勁，提供長端美
債支撐。雖然中東情勢反覆使油價維持高檔，通膨預
期仍偏高，但 Fed 官員多傾向先觀察，緩和短期升息
壓力。此外，高殖利率環境使 AI 與科技股估值面臨修
正壓力，股市震盪帶動避險資金流入美債，使 10 年期
美債殖利率自 10 月初 5.30%高位顯著回降至低點
5.04%後反彈。

圖 2 公債與投等債反彈較強，惟油價仍處高檔

資料來源：Bloomberg，2026/10/19 
◼ 9 月 CPI、PPI 升溫，消費與就業具韌性，年底升息預期仍存。不過，美債標售穩
健、Fed 官員偏向不急於在 10 月升息，股市震盪帶動避險買盤，10 年期殖利率
回降後反彈，債市自低檔修復，近期美國公債與投等債反彈幅度高於非投等債。 
◼ 近期美國投等債利差反彈相對受控，Q4「淨淨」發行量可望逐月下降，年底季節
性亦有利，技術面改善，企業獲利強勁，信用面穩健。美投等債殖利率仍處高檔，
高票息提供報酬緩衝，歷史經驗顯示，高殖利率通常伴隨較佳未來 12 個月報酬。 
◼ 產業配置上，金融債存續期間較短、利率敏感度較低，加上銀行貸款與存款維持
成長，資產品質穩健，防禦性相對較佳。科技債殖利率與OAS 高於整體投等債市
場，反映 AI 資本支出擴張推升發債供給，但科技企業財務體質仍佳，利差走寬後
收益補償提升。整體投資可偏向中短天期，依風險承受度與收益需求擇優布局。

隨長端殖利率回落，10 月以來主要債券自低點反彈，
其中美國公債與投等債對利率回落較為敏感 ， 反彈力道
高於受股市波動影響較大的非投等債。

▎信用利差走寬，美投等債相對具韌性 
近期債市明顯承壓，全球公債殖利率一度快速上行、利
率波動升高，加上企業債供給壓力偏高，信用利差也隨
之走寬。儘管近期美國投等債與非投等債 OAS 自低位
有所回升，但若以近 10 年歷史區間比較，目前仍處於
相對低檔。

圖 3 近 10 年美國投等債與非投等債 OAS 走勢

資料來源：Bloomberg，2026/10/19

而在這波信用利差走寬過程中，美國投等債 OAS 升幅
明顯低於其他主要信用債市場，仍展現較佳防禦性。

圖 4 近期美國投等債 OAS 升幅相對受控

資料來源：Bloomberg，2026/10/14

▎Q4 淨供給緩和 ， 投等債利差有望收斂 
企業債發行量維持高檔，仍對債市形成一定壓力，但進
入 Q4 後，美國投等債市場的供給壓力可望逐步緩和。
若進一步觀察「淨淨」發行量（net-net issuance ），
即扣除到期本金、贖回款與票息等回流資金後，市場實
際需消化的新增公司債供給，根據 J.P. Morgan 預估，
Q4「淨淨」 發行量將逐月下降 ， 新增供給對利差的壓力
可望減輕。

圖 5  Q4 美國投等債淨淨供給量預估逐月下降

資料來源：JPM，2026/10/2

同時，過去 4 年平均來看，美國投等債利差在 11 月、 
12 月偏向收斂，年底季節性有助支撐利差表現。供給
壓力下降與年底季節性相互配合 ， 使第4 季美國投等債
利差具備較有利的技術面條件。

誠信親切、專業創新 週報 
▎企業獲利預估強勁，支撐信用基本面 
近期殖利率上行雖受到通膨與貨幣政策預期影響 ， 部分
也反映美國經濟與企業獲利仍具韌性 。AI 基礎建設需求
與大型科技企業資本支出持續擴張 ， 加上企業利潤率維
持高檔 ， 成為獲利成長的重要支撐 。 標普500 企業獲利
自 2026 年以來明顯加速 ， 第1 季與第 2 季分別年成長
29%與 52%，Q3 與 Q4 預估仍達 30%與 28%。獲利
動能延續 ， 有助維持投等債發行公司的償債能力與財務
彈性，支撐高利率環境下的信用韌性。

圖 7 標普 500 企業 Q3、Q4 獲利年增率將維持高成長

資料來源：JPM，2026/10/2

▎高殖利率提供報酬緩衝 
目前美國投等債殖利率仍處相對高檔 ， 較高的殖利率可
提高票息緩衝，也為後續報酬提供較有利的起點。根據
JPM 自 1990 年以來的歷史資料顯示 ， 美國投等債當期
殖利率與未來 12 個月總報酬呈正向關係，殖利率較高
時，後續 12 個月報酬通常也較佳。在利率仍可能波動
的環境下，較高票息有助吸收部分價格波動，使目前的
收益條件較過去低殖利率時期更具支撐。 
圖 8 高殖利率通常伴隨較佳 12 個月報酬

資料來源：JPM，2026/10/12 
▎金融偏防禦，科技債補償提升 
綜合信用基本面、供需條件與收益水準，美國投等債整
體展望仍偏正向。在產業方面，金融債在殖利率接近整
體投等債市場的情況下，存續期間明顯較短。近期大型
銀行財報亦顯示，貸款與存款維持成長，整體資產品質
仍穩健，為金融債基本面提供支撐。科技債殖利率與
OAS 則高於整體市場，主要反映 AI 資本支出擴張帶動
發債供給快速增加。儘管融資需求升高，大型科技企業
整體資產負債表仍相對穩健 ， 利差走寬後的收益補償也
有所提升 。 整體配置上 ， 在利率仍具不確定性的環境下 ，
可偏向中短天期， 並依風險承受度與收益需求擇優布局 。

資料來源：Bloomberg，2026/10/15

## Document 576: 凱基投顧_2303 聯電_潘俊宏_20261028

### Page 1

十月 28, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R  Powered by the EFA Platform 1

動態更新 IC製造 ‧ 台灣

聯電 
(2303.TW /2303 TT)

強勁的成長在更遙遠的未來  
重要訊息 
聯電公布 3Q26 財報及上修資本支出計畫。  
評論及分析  
3Q26 獲利改善幅度不如預期 。聯電 3Q26 營收季增 8%至 742 億元，低於
凱基預估 5%，但符合市場共識，主要受惠產能利用率提升至 92%。毛利率
季增 1.5 個百分點至 34%，略低於預期。稅後淨利 164 億元，分別低於凱
基及市場預估 12.5%及 3%。展望 4Q26，公司預期晶圓出貨量將季增低個
位數百分比，ASP 持平，毛利率與 3Q26 相近，產能利用率則維持在略高於
90%的水準。 
加速擴充新產品產能 。聯電董事會於 7 月通過 2026-27 年 50 億美元資本支
出計畫，其中 2027 年資本支出主要用於無塵室及廠房擴建。此外，董事會
亦於今日 (10 月 28 日) 通過追加 32 億美元資本支出 (尚無明確時程，將視需
求及設備交期而定) ，用於擴充新規劃產品產能，包括矽光子月產能 1.8 萬片 
(新加坡 12i 廠)、矽電容月產能 2.2 萬片 (台南 12A 廠)，以及薄膜鈮酸鋰  
(TFLN)  調變器月產能 2 萬片 (主要位於 8 吋廠)。公司看好矽光子 (SiPh)  及先
進封裝需求的成長潛力，目前 12 吋光子積體電路 (PIC)  已支援 1.6T，並規
劃朝 3.2T 發展。新增產能預期自 2028 年底開始爬升， 2030 年起全面量
產。此外，聯電亦上修 AI 相關營收展望，由原先預期未來三年達 10 億美
元，提高至 2029 年營收占比 20-25% (25-30 億美元)。 
2027 年成長動能有限 。 由於產能利用率已處於高檔，聯電預期 2027 年出
貨成長將受限於產能吃緊。公司預期明年整體 ASP 將上漲 10%，主要來
自：(1)  2027 年第一季起調漲價格；(2)  製程 (產品) 組合改善。AI 相關 PMIC
及 FPGA 需求持續超過現有產能，有利於價格表現。然而，成本上升及折舊
增加 (我們預估 2027 年折舊年增 15%)  將限制毛利率改善幅度，但 EBITDA
利潤率仍有上行空間。  
投資建議 
考量產品價格上漲及 2028 年更積極擴產，我們分別上修 2027-28 年 EPS 預
估 1.9%及 22.1%。目標價由原先評價基礎延展至 2028 年，維持 18 倍目
標本益比 (歷史平均值加一個標準差) ，並將目標價上調至 160 元，維持「增
加持股」評等。 
投資風險  
矽光子發展或需求低於預期；成熟製程需求反轉。  
增加持股‧維持

ESG圖表定義 
項目 定義 說明 
能源使用 
直接與間接能源消耗總量(十億焦耳) 
- 公司運營範圍內消耗之能源總量 
- 能源使用總量 = 直接能源消耗總量 + 間接能源消耗量 
- 總能源使用量包括購買的能源、生產的能源 
- 就公用事業而言，輸電/電網損耗為其業務活動之一部分，被視為總能源消耗，數據不包括
為滿足能源使用而生產的電力(公用事業為出售而生產) 
- 就公用事業而言，用於能源生產的煤、天然氣或核能等原料不屬於「總能源使用」項下

購買之再生能源 
Primary再生能源購買總量(十億焦耳) 
- 公司各種來源之能源消耗量與購買的能源中屬於自然界可再生者(太陽能、風能、水能、生
物質能、地熱能)之量 
- 如無證據顯示再生能源由公司生產，則所報告的能源數據視為購買的再生能源

再生能源使用率 再生能源占總能源消耗量之比例  
CO2約當排放量 
直接 CO2與 CO2約當排放量(公噸) 
- 公司擁有或控制的來源的直接排放量(範圍 1排放量) 
- 相關氣體：二氧化碳(CO2)、甲烷(CH4)、一氧化二氮(N2O)、氫氟碳化物(HFCS)、全氟化合物
(PFCS)、六氟化硫(SF6)、三氟化氮(NF3)

CO2約當排放量營收比 
直接 CO2與 CO2約當排放量(公噸)銷售(百萬元)比 
- 公司擁有或控制的來源的直接排放量(範圍 1排放量) 
- 相關氣體：二氧化碳(CO2)、甲烷(CH4)、一氧化二氮(N2O)、氫氟碳化物(HFCS)、全氟化合物
(PFCS)、六氟化硫(SF6)、三氟化氮(NF3)

廢棄物總量 
廢棄物總量(公噸) 
- 總廢棄物 = 無害廢棄物 + 有害廢棄物 
- 僅考慮固體廢棄物，但如液體廢棄物以公噸為單位呈報，則會將其加入求得包含液體廢棄
物之總量 
- 對於採礦、石油與天然氣等行業，tailings、廢石、煤、飛灰等廢棄物亦考慮在內

廢棄物回收率 
公司呈報的廢棄物回收率 
- 廢棄物回收率 = 廢棄物回收量/總廢棄物*100 
- 廢棄物轉化為能源或經由廢棄物焚燒產生能源視為廢棄物回收 
- 經由堆肥回收的廢棄物視為回收之廢棄物

總取水量 
總取水量(立方公尺) 
- 由呈報組織直接或經水公司等中介機構從任何水源抽取的總水量 
- 水井、城鎮/公用事業/市政用水、河水、地表水等不同之水源均予以考慮

環保支出 
環保支出總金額 
- 所有用於環境保護，或防止、減少、控制環境因素、影響、危害的投資與支出，亦包括處
置、處理、衛生、清理支出

員工流動率 
員工流動率 
- 包括任何原因(自願或非自願)離開公司的員工，如辭職、退休、自然離職/死亡、醫療失
能、冗員、裁員、重組、解僱、裁減或定期合約到期 
- 員工流動率 = (離開之員工/平均員工人數)*100 
- 平均員工人數 = (本年末員工人數 + 去年末員工人數)/2 
- 本財年末員工數 = 上個財年末員工數 + 新員工數 - 離開之員工數

女性經理 
女性經理百分比 
- 女性經理占公司經理的百分比 
- 如有不同階層之百分比，如最高階、高階、中階、初階，則會考慮中階女性經理之百分比 
- 女性經理百分比 = 女性經理人數/經理總數*100

女性員工 
女性員工百分比 
- 女性員工占公司員工總數的百分比 
- 女性員工百分比 = 女性人數/員工總數*100

教育訓練總時數 
所有員工教育訓練總時數 
- 僅考慮員工教育訓練時數 
- 包括一般員工所有類型的教育訓練(如健康與安全、環境、急難事件救援、技能與職業發展) 
- 如果數據以天為單位，則乘以 8，係假設 1天 = 8小時工作

每位員工教育訓練時數 平均每年每位員工總教育訓練時數  
資料來源：Refinitiv，凱基

王翊安 
wade.wang@ctbcsis.com

PCB/半導體設備 
大量 (3167 TT)

首次 覆蓋  
買進(Buy) 
獲利大量，前途無量  
⚫ AI PCB 規格升級推升鑽孔需求，產能持續維持滿載  
⚫ 背鑽滲透率 提高， TM 放量帶動獲 利結構提升  
⚫ 一鑽導入 CCD 、1.6T 光模塊放量 帶動 CCD 占比提升  
9 月自結優於預期， TM4 出貨推升毛利率：  大量 9 月自結稅後淨利
3.5 億元 (YoY+542.9%) ，單月 EPS 達 3.82 元，大幅優於市場預期 ，依
自結數字推估， 9 月毛利率已達 54~55% ，主要受惠高毛利產品 TM4 開
始出貨，加上產品組合轉向高階 CCD 機種。展望 4Q26 ，隨外包月產
能提升至 50 台，我們預估 4Q26 營收可達 42.1 億元 (QoQ+14.6%, 
YoY+171.7%) ，毛利率提升至 52.3% ，調整後 EPS 為 12.13 元。  
AI PCB 規格升級推升鑽孔需求： 隨 AI PCB 層數、面積與孔密度提升，
一 鑽段 因板 厚增 加使 鑽孔 時間 拉長 ；背 鑽段 則因 傳輸 速度 提升 、對 殘
銅(Stub) 的容忍度降低，帶動高階 CCD 背鑽機需求。目前訂單已排至
2Q27 ，且部分客戶已預約至 2H27 出機，預期產能將持續供不應求。  
TM4 量 測 機 放 量 ， 搭 配 比 例 提 升 推 升 獲 利 ：厚度量測儀 (Thickness 
Metrology, TM) 可量測 PCB 之 Z 軸深度，使背鑽製程能精準去除殘銅，
且獨家 搭配公司自家的高階 CCD 背鑽機，有助於鞏固高階背鑽機市占
率。公司於 2026 年開始出貨四軸 TM4，單價為前一代的 1.5~2 倍；客
戶 CCD 鑽孔與 TM 拉貨比重提升到 5:1 ，未來可望進一步提升。我們
預估 TM 將貢獻 2026/2027/2028 年 EPS 分別為 4.32/27.28/42.47 元。  
CCD 鑽孔機與成型機滲透率同步提升 ：  (1) 一鑽通孔段精度要求提高 ，
且高階 AI PCB 報廢成本高，我們預期客戶將自 4Q26 起於一鑽導入高
階 CCD 鑽孔機； (2)1.6T 光模塊 採用之 成型機須搭載 CCD 對位，隨
1.6T 於 2027 年量產，將帶動 CCD 成型機需求。我們預估 2027 年高階
機種出貨占比將達 70% ，有助於推升 ASP 與毛利率。  
自有與外包產能同步擴張，支撐出貨成長：  公司自有月產能由 3M26
的 350 台提升至目前的 400 台；外包月產能 2026 年底 達 50 台。外包
產能 有助於釋出自有產能，集中生產高階 CCD 設備 與 TM。我們預估
2026/2027/2028 年調整後 EPS 分別為 31.73/88.07/126.19 元；以 2027
年調整後 EPS 及 23 倍本益比推估目標價為 2,000 元，建議買進。  
風險因子： TM 拉貨不如預期、同業競爭加劇 。

 公司簡介： 大量成立於 1980 年，總部位於桃園八德， 2013 年於台灣證交所上市，生產基地位於台灣八德及中國江蘇南京、漣水。公司主要產品為 PCB
鑽孔機、成型機與厚度量測機 (TM) 。以 2025 年營收結構來看，高階 CCD 機
種占比約 46%，標準機種約 42%，半導體設備約 7%，玻璃邊緣塗佈機約 5%；
銷售地區以中國為主，占 68.7% ，其次為東南亞 21.7% 、台灣 9.6% 。公司自
行開發控制器與軟體，是 ±2mil 背鑽量測解決方案的唯一供應商；半導體事
業已於 2026 年 8 月 1 日分拆為大量先進智能。 2025 年員工數約 618 人。  
圖表一、 大量 營收產品組合趨勢

資料來源： Bloomberg 、中信投顧彙整

板廠客戶資本支出上修 ， 鑽孔設備直接受惠 ： 大量的客戶以台系 PCB 板廠為
主，包含欣興 (3037) 、臻鼎 -KY(4958) 、金像電 (2368) 等大型板廠。受惠 AI 伺
服器需求強勁，主要客戶持續加碼資本支出，三家合計 2Q26 資 本 支 出 達
245.5 億元，季增 27.1% 、年增 51.7% （見圖表 二） ，近四季累計785.4 億元，
年增 43.6% 。展望 2026 年，根據台灣電路板協會 (TPCA) 資料，全年 PCB 相
關資本支出規模將達 2,300 億元。其中欣興、臻鼎、金像電的 2026 年資本支
出分別為 540 億元、逾 800 億元及 190 億元，較年初規劃的 254 億元、逾
500 億元及 170 億元均大幅上修，三家合計逾 1,530 億元，占產業資本支出
逾 6 成，且皆於指出 2027 年資本支出仍將維持高檔。鑽孔設備是板廠擴產
的必要投資，且 AI PCB 的單片鑽孔時間持續拉長，我們預期大量將直接受
惠於主要客戶的擴產循環。

3 
大量的鑽孔設備需求主要來自兩段製程： (1) 一鑽通孔段，這是 PCB 製程的
必要工序，鑽孔方式分雷射鑽孔與機械鑽孔，大量供應機械鑽孔設備； (2) 背
鑽，隨 AI 伺服器的高速傳輸需求提升，背鑽已成高階 PCB 的關鍵製程。  
(1) 一鑽通孔段： 多層板壓合後，各層線路被絕緣介電層隔開，需要用機械鑽
針貫穿板材形成通孔 (PTH)，再經鍍銅讓各層導通。 AI PCB 朝高層數 HLC 與
高階 HDI 混壓的方向發展， HLC 核心層由過去的 20~24 層提升到 40 層以上
(見圖表 三)。層數 增加 使板厚上升，單片 板須 分段鑽孔，可疊板 加工 的片數
也減少；加上高階 CCL 材料較硬，鑽針耗損加快、加工速度下降，單位鑽孔
時間因此明顯拉長。同時，每櫃 PCB 的面積與孔數持續增加，在孔數增加 ×
單孔時間拉長的效果下，板廠對機械鑽孔的產能需求將倍數成長，將帶動大
量鑽孔機需求成長。  
圖表 三、主流 AI GPU/ASIC 不同世代 PCB 規格

資料來源： 中信投顧整理

圖表 四、PCB 通孔示意圖

資料來源： WDF

### Page 4

個股報告  
                                       2 0 2 6 / 10 / 19

4 
(2)背鑽 (Backdrill) ：PCB 完成通孔並鍍銅後，整支孔都會導電，但訊號不一
定要連通每一層。例如訊號只需要從第 1 層走到第 8 層，孔銅卻一路延伸到
第 30 層，第 8 層以下這段沒用到的孔銅稱為 Stub( 殘銅 )。Stub 在高頻傳輸
下會造成訊號反射與共振，使訊號損耗增加； Stub 越長，影響越明顯。傳輸
速率由 112G 提升到 224G 以後，可容忍的 Stub 長度持續縮短，因此需要從
板子背面把多餘的孔銅鑽 除，也就是背鑽。背鑽必須對準既有通孔，並把鑽
孔深度控制在目標層附近，鑽太深會切斷訊號、鑽太淺則殘留過長，精度要
求明顯高於一鑽 通孔段 ，因此主要採用具影像對位功能的 CCD 鑽孔機。隨
AI 伺服器傳輸速率持續提升，背鑽滲透率與精度要求同步提高，等於在一鑽
之外多出一道高精度鑽孔工序，將帶動大量 CCD 背鑽機的需求。  
圖表 五、背鑽概念示意圖

資料來源： iPCB  
Rubin 背鑽精度要求提升， TM4 為 Z 軸深度控制的關鍵方案： 背鑽精度主
要看兩項指標。 (1) 對位度：背鑽孔要對準原本的通孔，目前業界要求背鑽孔
徑在原孔徑加 6 mil(D+6 mil) 以內，目前終端客戶尚未要求縮小到 D+4 mil ，
短期不是主要瓶頸。 (2) 殘銅 (Stub) 長度：這是 影 響訊號品質的關鍵，客 戶要
求也最嚴格。以 NVIDIA 為例， PCB 訊號頻率由 Blackwell 世代的 28GHz 提
升到 Rubin 世代的 56GHz，背鑽殘銅精度要求也隨之由 ±3 mil 收斂到 ±2 mil，
以避免殘銅干擾訊號（見圖表 六）。 
圖表六、 NVIDIA GPU 平台背鑽殘銅精度要求

資料來源：中信投顧整理

殘銅長度取決於背鑽在 Z 軸(深度 )的控制精度 ：AI PCB 面積擴大後，壓合產
生的翹曲 (Warpage) 會讓板內各孔位的實際深度不同 ， 而單片大板的孔數動輒
上萬，若用固定深度背鑽，很難把殘銅控制在 ±2 mil 以內。大量的厚度量測
儀(Thickness Metrology, TM) 在背鑽前先量測各孔內層銅層的實際深度 ， 再把
深度資料傳給背鑽機逐孔調整，讓殘銅控制在規格內。 TM 採用公司自行開
發的控制器與軟體，只能搭配自家的高階 CCD 背鑽機使用，並且現在主要
競爭同業大族、 Schmoll 並沒有相對應 TM 的 Z 軸量測解決方案，故我們認
爲背鑽在 PCB 製程的滲透率逐步提升下，大量將會在鑽孔機市場持續獲得

### Page 5

個股報告  
                                       2 0 2 6 / 10 / 19

5 
更多市佔率。公司先前的 TM2/TM3 為單軸設備，受限於 Throughput ；2026
年開始出貨的四軸 TM4，Throughput 提升為前一代的 4 倍，單價為前一代的
1.5~2 倍，毛利率超過 70% 。我們預期 TM4 將為 2027 年的出貨主力。  
在 CCD 負責 XY 軸對位、 TM 負責 Z 軸深度量測的分工下，背鑽精度要求提
高，同步推升 TM 的需求。客戶採購 CCD 背鑽機與 TM 的比例已由過去的
8:1 提高到目前的 5~6:1 ，未來可望往 2:1 發展。 TM 占比提升，將帶動大量
的產品組合轉佳與獲利成長，我們認為這是大量 2027/2028 年最主要的成長
動能。我們針對 TM 出貨做 2027 年 EPS 的情境分析 (見圖表七 )，並考量 TM
生產時間與 CCD 機種相近 、 須共用自有產能 ， 預估TM 將貢獻 2026/2027/2028
年 EPS 分別為 4.32/27.28/42.47 元，占整體調整後 EPS 約 14%/31%/34% 。 
圖表七、 CCD 背鑽 :TM 出貨比例 /TM 月產能之情境分析表

資料來源：中信投顧整理

CCD 機種滲透率提升，產品組合轉向高階： CCD 機種是在鑽孔機與成型機
上加裝影像對位系統（ Charge -Coupled Device, CCD ） 。加工前先用鏡頭辨識
板面上的對位標記 ， 依線路的實際位置修正 XY 軸 （平面） 的加工座標，CCD
機種單價約為標準機台的 2~3 倍 。 大量的CCD 機種分為 CCD 成型機與 CCD
鑽孔機，其中成型與鑽孔約各占一半。  
圖表八、大量 PCB 設備產品線

資料來源：公司

### Page 6

個股報告  
                                       2 0 2 6 / 10 / 19

6 
(1)CCD 成型機： 成型是 PCB 製程在測試前的最後階段，負責把整片大板依
設計外形切割成單片電路板 。 一般 PCB 的公差標準較寬鬆，用標準成型機即
可。光模塊 PCB 的尺寸小，焊墊 (Bonding Pad) 到板邊的距離必須精準控制。
800G 光模塊的精度要求在 50µm 以內，1.6T 進一步縮小到 25µm 以內，需要
用 CCD 成型機依線路實際位置修正切割路徑。隨 800G 光模塊進入量產、
1.6T 預計 2027 年量產，預計 CCD 成型機的出貨動能將不輸鑽孔機。 大量 在
CCD 成型機市場的市占 40% 以上 ，光模塊成型機更高， 為市場領導者。  
(2)CCD 鑽孔機： 背鑽要對準原本的通孔，因此必須使用 CCD 鑽孔機。分工
上，CCD 負責 XY 軸對位， TM 負責量測各孔的 Z 軸實際深度，背鑽機再依
TM 的深度資料逐孔控深加工。目前每台 TM4 約帶動 5~6 台 CCD 鑽孔機的
需求。至於一鑽通孔段，過去多使用標準機台；但隨 SerDes 速率由 112G 提
升到 224G ，PCB 佈線密度提高，線寬線距與焊墊 (Pad) 同步縮小，孔環寬度
縮減，通孔段可容忍的鑽偏程度跟著降低，一鑽的 XY 對位精度要求也開始
比照背鑽。公司簡報也已將高階 PCB 的通孔鑽孔列為 CCD 鑽孔機的獨立應
用，目前已有客戶在一鑽階段導入。加上高階 AI PCB 單片價值高、鑽孔不
良的報廢成本大，我們預期一鑽導入 CCD 將成為明確趨勢， CCD 鑽孔機的
應用將由背鑽延伸到一鑽。  
綜合上述，我們預期 CCD 機種滲透率將持續提升。 CCD 機種的單價與毛利
率 均 明 顯 高 於 標 準 機 台 ： 標 準 機 台 毛 利 率 約15~20% ， 高 階 CCD 機 種 約
45~60% 。我們預估高階機種（含 TM）的出貨占比將由公司 2026 年目標的
60% ，進一步提升到 2027 年的 70% ，帶動毛利率由 1H26 的 43.5% 提升至
2027 年的 55.9% (見圖表 九)。 
圖表 九、大量設備產能分配預估及對應毛利率

資料來源：中信投顧整理

訂單滿載至 2027 年 ， 外 包 釋 出 自 有 產 能 轉 做 高 階 ：目 前 客 戶 訂 單 已 排 至
2Q27 ，且已有客戶預約 2H27 出機，高階 CCD 機種與標準機台的交期分別
約 4 個月及 3 個月，我們預期大量將維持滿載至 2027 年底。為因應需求，
公司自有月產能由 3M26 法說會時的 300 台提升至目前的 400 台（若全數生
產高階 CCD 機種約 300 台） ，外包月產能也由 20~30 台提升至 4Q26 的 50
台。展望 2027 年，我們預估外包月產能將於 1H27 達 80 台、 2H27 達 150
台，目前已經啟用第二家外包廠，建廠完後需要 3~6 個月訓練期以達公司要
求。外包廠目前受技術轉移限制，僅能生產標準機台，但可釋出自有產能，

### Page 7

個股報告  
                                       2 0 2 6 / 10 / 19

7 
讓自有產能轉做高階 CCD 機種，公司的目標是將自有產能全數轉做高階機
種，標準機台則交由外包生產。  
在自有產能配置上 ， CCD 設備與 TM 的生產時間均較標準機台長，且 TM 同
樣使用自有產能，實際產品配置仍須依客戶需求調整。我們認為，隨背鑽 Z
軸 量 測 需 求 提 升 ， 公 司 將 有 機 會 把 更 多 自 有 產 能 配 置 到 單 價 與 毛 利 更 高 的
TM，成為未來獲利的潛在增量。  
大量先進智能分拆獨立，為後續上市櫃預作準備： 大量於 2022 年將自身在
高精度量測、運動控制與視覺檢測的技術延伸至半導體設備領域。董事會於
2026 年 3 月 31 日通過將半導體事業分割至 100% 持股的子公司「大量先進
智能」 ，並於2026 年 8 月 1 日完成分拆。公司規劃後續推動大量先進智能上
市櫃，掛牌後大量預計仍將維持約 60% 持股。營收方面，受惠先進封裝擴產，
半導體事業營收由 2022 年的 0.3 億元成長至 2025 年的 3.1 億元，2022~2025
年營收年複合成長率 (CAGR) 達 117% ，目前約占公司整體營收 5~8% 。 
產品涵蓋前中後段製程 ， CoWoS 為主要營收來源： 大量先進智能的產品包含
量測 (Metrology) 、自動光 學檢測 (AOI) 與自動化設備，應用範圍涵蓋前段晶圓
製造、中段 CoW 與後段 oS 製程。 圖表 十、十 一為 2025 年營收結構：

隨 CoWoS 封裝尺寸持續放大，翹曲 (Warpage) 造成的厚度、高度差與邊緣偏
移等問題日益明顯，量測與檢測的重要性隨之提升。公司沿用 PCB 事業 TM
量測機的自研控制器與軟體，除了檢測缺陷外，也能量化缺陷與翹曲程度，
這是切入半導體量測的關鍵。

封測擴產帶動成長 ， CPO 有望成為下一個動能 ： 公司目前已通過晶圓代工廠
認證，封測廠客戶也持續擴大導入。隨晶圓代工與封測產能同步提升，預期
封測端營收成長將快於晶圓代工端，且公司已取得明年訂單。此外，公司也
與客戶合作開發 CoPoS 與共同封裝光學 (CPO) 相關設備， CPO 檢測分選設備
目前處於驗證階段，預期 2027 年需求將逐步顯現。整體而言，我們看好大
量先進智能受惠先進封裝擴產，營運可望維持高速成長

綜上所述，大量 PCB 與半導體業務同步高速成長，我們預估 2026/2027/2028
年調整後 EPS 分別為 31.73/88.07/126.19 元(YoY +291%/+178%/+43%) 。我們
看好以下四點： (1) 背鑽滲透率持續提升； (2) 公司 Z 軸量測方案 TM 領先同
業，且僅能搭配自家高階 CCD 背鑽機，有助於提升鑽孔機市占率； (3) 一鑽
製程導入 CCD 鑽孔機，加上 1.6T 光模塊採用 CCD 成型機，帶動產品組合
持續轉向高階 CCD 機種； (4) 大量先進智能分拆後，半導體業務持續成長，
有助於公司評價由 PCB 設備向半導體設備靠攏 。 在多重成長動能下 ， 我們給
予 2027 年調整後 EPS 23 倍本益比，位於公司近兩年預估本益比 (Forward 
PER) 區間上緣，推估目標價為 2,000 元， 潛在上漲空間 89.57% ，建議買進。
